CISA KEV: CVE-2025-64328 — Sangoma FreePBX OS Command Injection Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-02-03 1 use case 0 techniques 2 kill-chain phases detected

Sangoma FreePBX Endpoint Manager contains an OS command injection vulnerability that could allow for a post-authentication command injection by an authenticated known user via the testconnection -> check_ssh_connect() function. An attacker can leverage this vulnerability to potentially obtain remote access to the system as an asterisk user. Vendor: Sangoma, Product: FreePBX . Federal patch due: 2026-02-24.

ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process

[LLM] DynoWiper / ZOV wiper known-bad SHA-1 hash execution Installation High
Hunts execution of the five DynoWiper / ZOV wiper binaries documented by ESET against the Polish energy victim and the earlier Ukraine ZOV incident. Hash match is the most deterministic indicator we have for this Sandworm sub-campaign. Rationale: All five SHA-1 hashes are pulled directly from the ESET IOC table for DynoWiper (Win32/KillFiles.NMO) and ZOV wiper (Win32/KillFiles.NMJ). Hash-based detection is highest-fidelity for wipers because Sandworm rebuilds them between campaigns.
ATT&CKT1485 T1561.001
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
let DynoWiperZovSha1 = dynamic(["472CA448F82A7FF6F373A32FDB9586FD7C38B631","4F8E9336A784A196353023133E0F8FA54F6A92E2","4EC3C90846AF6B79EE1A5188EEFA3FD21F6D4CF6","86596A5C5B05A8BFBD14876DE7404702F7D0D61B","69EDE7E341FD26FA0577692B601D80CB44778D93"]);
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where SHA1 in~ (DynoWiperZovSha1) or InitiatingProcessSHA1 in~ (DynoWiperZovSha1)
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine, SHA1, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath
| order by Timestamp desc
let DynoWiperZovSha1 = dynamic(["472ca448f82a7ff6f373a32fdb9586fd7c38b631","4f8e9336a784a196353023133e0f8fa54f6a92e2","4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6","86596a5c5b05a8bfbd14876de7404702f7d0d61b","69ede7e341fd26fa0577692b601d80cb44778d93"]);
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where tolower(SHA1) in (DynoWiperZovSha1)

| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Executable dropped into C:\inetpub\pub\ shared directory Delivery High
Hunts the article-specific staging behaviour where Sandworm dropped schtask.exe, schtask2.exe and <redacted>_update.exe to C:\inetpub\pub\ — a directory under the IIS root that the actor used as a domain-shared drop point. Legitimate Windows administration almost never writes PE files to this path. Rationale: Article explicitly names C:\inetpub\pub\ as the drop location for schtask.exe / schtask2.exe / <redacted>_update.exe. IIS publishing roots are not normal admin script targets, so PE creation there is a high-fidelity wiper-staging signal.
ATT&CKT1570 T1105
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileRenamed","FileModified")
| where FolderPath startswith @"C:\inetpub\pub\"
| where FileName endswith ".exe" or FileName endswith ".dll" or FileName endswith ".scr" or FileName endswith ".bat" or FileName endswith ".ps1"
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA1, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName, RequestAccountName, RequestSourceIP
| order by Timestamp desc
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where TargetFilePath startswith @"C:\inetpub\pub\"
| where TargetFileExtension in~ ("exe","dll","scr","bat","ps1")
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, TargetFileSHA1, TargetFileSHA256
| order by TimeGenerated desc

id: 7c2a91d4-3b9f-4e8c-a1d2-6e3b5c4a1f8d
description: Detects creation of executable / script files in C:\inetpub\pub\, the staging directory used by Sandworm to deploy DynoWiper to a Polish energy victim.
references:
  - https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/dynowiper-update-technical-analysis-attribution/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21

  - attack.t1105

  category: file_event
  product: windows

    TargetFilename|startswith: 'C:\inetpub\pub\'

level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:11 (@TargetFilename:(*\\inetpub\\pub\\* OR *\\Inetpub\\Pub\\*)) (@TargetFilename:(*.exe OR *.dll OR *.scr OR *.bat OR *.ps1))

[LLM] DynoWiper schtask.exe / *_update.exe execution from C:\inetpub\pub\ Installation High
Hunts process execution of the article-named binaries (schtask.exe, schtask2.exe, *_update.exe) when they launch from C:\inetpub\pub\. Schtask masquerades as schtasks.exe — the legitimate Windows binary uses an 's' at the end, the wiper drops the 's'. Rationale: Article names the three binaries (schtask.exe, schtask2.exe, <redacted>_update.exe) AND the staging path C:\inetpub\pub\. The combination of exact filename plus inetpub path is article-specific and effectively zero-FP. schtask.exe (no trailing s) is a deliberate masquerade of the Windows schtasks.exe binary.
ATT&CKT1485 T1036.005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FolderPath startswith @"C:\inetpub\pub\"
| where FileName in~ ("schtask.exe","schtask2.exe") or FileName endswith "_update.exe"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, AccountDomain, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine, SHA1, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where NewProcessName startswith @"C:\inetpub\pub\"
| where NewProcessName endswith @"\schtask.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\schtask2.exe" or NewProcessName endswith "_update.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, SubjectUserName, SubjectDomainName, NewProcessName, CommandLine, ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Mass file-content overwrite by single non-system process from non-standard path Actions on Objectives Medium
Hunts the DynoWiper impact phase — the wiper overwrites file content with a 16-byte buffer of random data generated once at start of execution. Detects a single non-system, non-Program-Files process touching an unusually high count of distinct files within a short window. Rationale: Article specifies DynoWiper's destructive phase overwrites every reachable file content with a 16-byte random buffer; the resulting telemetry shape is one process emitting hundreds-to-thousands of FileModified events in seconds. Excluding C:\Windows\ and C:\Program Files anchors the hunt away from updaters/installers.
ATT&CKT1485 T1561.001
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
let WindowMinutes = 5m;
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where ActionType in ("FileModified","FileCreated")
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFolderPath !startswith @"C:\Windows\"
| where InitiatingProcessFolderPath !startswith @"C:\Program Files"
| summarize FileCount = dcount(FolderPath), SampleFiles = make_set(FolderPath, 20), SampleProc = any(InitiatingProcessCommandLine)
    by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessId, bin(Timestamp, WindowMinutes)
| where FileCount > 200
| order by Timestamp desc
let WindowMinutes = 5m;
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventType in ("FileModified","FileCreated")
| where isnotempty(ActingProcessName)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where not(ActingProcessName startswith @"C:\Windows\")
| where not(ActingProcessName startswith @"C:\Program Files")
| summarize FileCount = dcount(TargetFilePath), SampleFiles = make_set(TargetFilePath, 20), SampleCmd = any(ActingProcessCommandLine)
    by DvcHostname, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessId, bin(TimeGenerated, WindowMinutes)
| where FileCount > 200
| order by TimeGenerated desc
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:11 -@Image:(C:\\Windows\\* OR C:\\Program\ Files* OR c:\\windows\\* OR c:\\program\ files*)
#event_simpleName=FileWritten
| NOT ContextImageFileName=/^[cC]:\\Windows\\/
| NOT ContextImageFileName=/^[cC]:\\Program Files/
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ContextImageFileName, ContextProcessId], function=count(as=FileCount))
| FileCount > 200
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` dc(Filesystem.file_path) as FileCount values(Filesystem.file_path) as Files min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","written") Filesystem.process_path!="C:\\Windows\\*" Filesystem.process_path!="C:\\Program Files*" by Filesystem.dest Filesystem.process_name Filesystem.process_path Filesystem.user span=5m | where FileCount > 200 | `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false dc(Filesystem.file_path) as FileCount values(Filesystem.file_path) as Files min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","written") Filesystem.process_path!="C:\\Windows\\*" Filesystem.process_path!="C:\\Program Files*" by Filesystem.dest Filesystem.process_name Filesystem.process_path Filesystem.user span=5m | where FileCount > 200 | `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

title: Scheduled Task Creation with Suspicious Image / Encoded Args
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0d
status: experimental
description: |
    schtasks.exe /create with payload-shaped action — points at

    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1053/005/
author: Clankerusecase

    - attack.execution

    category: process_creation
    product: windows

            - 'cmd.exe'

        OR Processes.process="*\AppData\*")

    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes

        OR Processes.process="*rundll32*" OR Processes.process="*-enc*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64*" OR Processes.process="*\Users\Public*"
        OR Processes.process="*\AppData\*")

| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT

| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-1281")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-1281")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

[LLM] GhostChat C2/staging infrastructure contact (hitpak.org, buildthenations.info, fkclb.com) Command & Control High
Hunts any endpoint resolving or contacting the GhostChat / ClickFix campaign infrastructure tied to the Pakistan-targeted spyware operation. While the Android payload itself won't show on EDR, the same actor's ClickFix attack delivers a Windows implant that beacons to the same C2 estate, so DNS/proxy hits on these domains from Windows hosts are high-confidence pivots. Rationale: Domains hitpak.org, buildthenations.info, and fkclb.com are the dual-use C2/staging hosts shared between the Android GhostChat payload and the same actor's ClickFix Windows implant per ESET's writeup; any Windows endpoint contact is anomalous because the brand impersonates a Pakistani government site and has no legitimate enterprise use. Cross-checked with WeLiveSecurity blog and ESET telemetry tags. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/love-actually-fake-dating-app-used-lure-targeted-spyware-campaign-pakistan/
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004 T1583.001
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Web.Web
let GhostChatDomains = dynamic(["hitpak.org","buildthenations.info","fkclb.com"]);
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteUrl has_any (GhostChatDomains) or RemoteUrl endswith ".hitpak.org" or RemoteUrl endswith ".buildthenations.info" or RemoteUrl endswith ".fkclb.com"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| order by Timestamp desc
let GhostChatDomains = dynamic(["hitpak.org","buildthenations.info","fkclb.com"]);
union isfuzzy=true
  (DnsEvents
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where Name has_any (GhostChatDomains) or Name endswith ".hitpak.org" or Name endswith ".buildthenations.info" or Name endswith ".fkclb.com"
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=Computer, ClientIP, Domain=Name, QueryType=QueryTypeName, Source="DnsEvents"),
  (CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where RequestURL has_any (GhostChatDomains) or DestinationHostName has_any (GhostChatDomains)
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=coalesce(SourceHostName, DeviceExternalID), ClientIP=SourceIP, Domain=coalesce(DestinationHostName, RequestURL), QueryType=DeviceAction, Source="CommonSecurityLog"),
  (ImWebSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where Url has_any (GhostChatDomains) or DstHostname has_any (GhostChatDomains)
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=SrcHostname, ClientIP=SrcIpAddr, Domain=coalesce(DstHostname, HttpHostName), QueryType=HttpMethod, Source="ImWebSession")
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] GhostChat C2 beacon URL pattern: hitpak.org/page.php?tynor=<host>sss<user> exfil High
Detects the exact GhostChat / ClickFix C2 beacon URL template observed by ESET, where the malware exfiltrates host+username via the tynor query parameter delimited by the literal string 'sss'. This URL shape is unique to the campaign and is not used by any legitimate web service. Rationale: The URL template `/page.php?tynor=<host>sss<user>` is a malware-specific exfiltration shape — the literal token `tynor` and the `sss` delimiter between the two encoded fields are unique strings called out by ESET. Combining the template with the three known C2 domains gives near-zero false-positive rate. Cross-referenced via ESET WeLiveSecurity blog. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/love-actually-fake-dating-app-used-lure-targeted-spyware-campaign-pakistan/
ATT&CKT1041 T1071.001 T1567
Data sourcesWeb.Web Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteUrl matches regex @"(?i)(hitpak\.org|buildthenations\.info|fkclb\.com)/page\.php\?tynor=[^&]*sss[^&]*"
   or RemoteUrl has "page.php?tynor=" and RemoteUrl has "sss"
| extend BeaconHost = extract(@"(?i)tynor=([^s&]+)sss", 1, RemoteUrl)
| extend BeaconUser = extract(@"(?i)sss([^&]+)", 1, RemoteUrl)
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, BeaconHost, BeaconUser
| order by Timestamp desc
union isfuzzy=true
  (CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where (RequestURL matches regex @"(?i)(hitpak\.org|buildthenations\.info|fkclb\.com)/page\.php\?tynor=[^&]*sss[^&]*")
         or (RequestURL has "page.php?tynor=" and RequestURL has "sss")
    | extend BeaconHost = extract(@"(?i)tynor=([^s&]+)sss", 1, RequestURL)
    | extend BeaconUser = extract(@"(?i)sss([^&]+)", 1, RequestURL)
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=coalesce(SourceHostName, DeviceExternalID), ClientIP=SourceIP, User=coalesce(SourceUserName, AccountName), URL=RequestURL, UA=RequestClientApplication, BeaconHost, BeaconUser, Source="CommonSecurityLog"),
  (ImWebSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where (Url matches regex @"(?i)(hitpak\.org|buildthenations\.info|fkclb\.com)/page\.php\?tynor=[^&]*sss[^&]*")
         or (Url has "page.php?tynor=" and Url has "sss")
    | extend BeaconHost = extract(@"(?i)tynor=([^s&]+)sss", 1, Url)
    | extend BeaconUser = extract(@"(?i)sss([^&]+)", 1, Url)
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=SrcHostname, ClientIP=SrcIpAddr, User=SrcUsername, URL=Url, UA=HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent, BeaconHost, BeaconUser, Source="ImWebSession"),
  (W3CIISLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where csUriStem endswith "page.php" and csUriQuery has "tynor=" and csUriQuery has "sss"
    | extend BeaconHost = extract(@"(?i)tynor=([^s&]+)sss", 1, csUriQuery)
    | extend BeaconUser = extract(@"(?i)sss([^&]+)", 1, csUriQuery)
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=Computer, ClientIP=cIP, User=csUserName, URL=strcat(csUriStem, "?", csUriQuery), UA=csUserAgent, BeaconHost, BeaconUser, Source="W3CIISLog")
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

[LLM] ScreenConnect client beaconing to ClawdBot attacker relay (meeting.bulletmailer.net:8041) Command & Control High
Detects ScreenConnect client/shell binaries connecting to the attacker-controlled relay infrastructure used by the fake ClawdBot VS Code extension campaign. Catches both DNS-based and IP/port-based beacons to the weaponised RMM tenant. Rationale: Anchors on three attacker-owned domains and two IPs named in the article plus the non-standard ScreenConnect relay port 8041 — combination of weaponised ScreenConnect process names with a specific port/host gives effectively zero FPs outside the campaign. Article confirms attacker generated a pre-configured ScreenConnect client pointing at meeting.bulletmailer.net:8041.
ATT&CKT1219 T1071.001 T1572
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Network_Resolution.DNS
let C2Hosts = dynamic(["meeting.bulletmailer.net","clawdbot.getintwopc.site","darkgptprivate.com"]);
let C2IPs = dynamic(["178.16.54.253","179.43.176.32"]);

| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteIP in (C2IPs)
   or (isnotempty(RemoteUrl) and RemoteUrl has_any (C2Hosts))
   or (RemotePort == 8041 and InitiatingProcessFileName startswith "ScreenConnect.")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteIP, RemoteUrl, RemotePort
| order by Timestamp desc

[LLM] Weaponised ScreenConnect install path with attacker instance GUID 083e4d30c7ea44f7 Installation High
Detects file or process events under the unique ScreenConnect Client instance GUID (083e4d30c7ea44f7) baked into the attacker-generated installer. Every ScreenConnect tenant generates a unique GUID — observing this specific one anywhere in the estate is unambiguous campaign evidence. Rationale: The ScreenConnect install directory name embeds the tenant's unique instance ID (article: 'C:\Program Files (x86)\ScreenConnect Client (083e4d30c7ea44f7)\'). This GUID is opaque, attacker-generated, and will not appear in any legitimate corporate ScreenConnect deployment — single-string match is effectively zero-FP.

[LLM] DWrite.dll Rust sideloader dropped outside Windows directory (ClawdBot redundant payload) Installation High
Detects the malicious Rust-compiled DWrite.dll (SHA256 d1e0c2...) used as a redundant DLL-sideload payload by the ClawdBot extension to fetch a backup payload from Dropbox. Catches both the file drop and the load into the ScreenConnect host process. Rationale: DWrite.dll is a legitimate Microsoft DirectWrite library that almost exclusively lives under C:\Windows\. A copy outside that path matching the article's SHA256 (d1e0c2...) or located inside the attacker's ScreenConnect GUID dir / %TEMP%\Lightshot is a textbook DLL sideload — confirmed by Cynet, Elastic, and Ikarus VT verdicts cited in the article.
ATT&CKT1574.002 T1105 T1027.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
let BadHash = "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea";
union isfuzzy=true
  (DeviceImageLoadEvents
     | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
     | where FileName =~ "DWrite.dll"
     | where not(FolderPath startswith @"C:\Windows\")
     | where SHA256 == BadHash
        or InitiatingProcessFileName startswith "ScreenConnect."
        or FolderPath has "083e4d30c7ea44f7"
        or FolderPath has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"
     | project Timestamp, DeviceName, Source="ImageLoad", FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
               InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine),
  (DeviceFileEvents
     | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
     | where FileName =~ "DWrite.dll"
     | where SHA256 == BadHash
        or FolderPath has "083e4d30c7ea44f7"
        or FolderPath has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"
     | project Timestamp, DeviceName, Source="FileWrite", FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
               InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath=InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine)
| order by Timestamp desc
let BadHash = "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea";

| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where TargetFileName =~ "DWrite.dll"
| where not(TargetFilePath startswith_cs @"C:\Windows\")
| where TargetFileSHA256 == BadHash
    or TargetFilePath has "083e4d30c7ea44f7"
    or TargetFilePath has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"
    or ActingProcessName startswith "ScreenConnect."
| project TimeGenerated, Host=DvcHostname, User=ActorUsername, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256, Proc=ActingProcessName, Cmd=ActingProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] VS Code (Code.exe/node) drops payload to %TEMP%\Lightshot staging directory Delivery High
Hunts for the ClawdBot extension's staging step where the VS Code extension host writes downloaded executables/DLLs into %TEMP%\Lightshot before launching them detached with windowsHide. Catches both primary (Code.exe) and fallback (Dropbox MSI) drops. Rationale: Article verbatim: `const INIT_DIR = path.join(process.env.TEMP, 'Lightshot');` and `spawn(exePath, [], { detached: true, stdio: 'ignore', windowsHide: true }).unref()`. The combination of VS Code's Code.exe/node.exe writing into %TEMP%\Lightshot — a directory unrelated to legitimate VS Code use — plus the article's two file hashes and filenames (Code.exe, DWrite.dll, zoomupdate.msi) gives a high-fidelity multi-anchor hunt.
ATT&CKT1059.007 T1105 T1564.003 T1204.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes
let BadHashes = dynamic(["e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2","d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea"]);
let StagingDrops = DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where FolderPath has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"
    | where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("Code.exe","node.exe")
         or SHA256 in (BadHashes)
         or FileName in~ ("Code.exe","DWrite.dll","zoomupdate.msi","ffmpeg.dll","libEGL.dll","v8_context_snapshot.bin")
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessParentFileName;
let DetachedExec = DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where FolderPath has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"
         or InitiatingProcessFolderPath has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine;
union StagingDrops, DetachedExec
| order by Timestamp desc
let BadHashes = dynamic(["e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2","d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea"]);

              Path=TargetFilePath, FileName=TargetFileName, SHA256=TargetFileSHA256,

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
Email attachment opened from external sender Delivery High
Attachment delivery + execution by recipient — most common malware initial access.
ATT&CKT1566.001 T1204.002
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Endpoint.Processes EmailAttachmentInfo DeviceProcessEvents
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MalAttachments = EmailAttachmentInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | project NetworkMessageId, RecipientEmailAddress,
              AttachmentFileName = FileName, AttachmentSHA256 = SHA256;
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("OUTLOOK.EXE","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                      "mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe")
| join kind=inner MalAttachments on $left.AccountUpn == $right.RecipientEmailAddress
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, AttachmentFileName, AttachmentSHA256
// Sentinel doesn't have an attachment-hash table. Best approximation:
// OfficeActivity Send/Receive activity + correlate to host process spawn.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MailWithAttach = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation in~ ("Send","Receive")
    | where isnotempty(MessageId)
    | project EmailTime = TimeGenerated, MailboxOwnerUPN, Sender,
              Recipients, Subject, MessageId;
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName endswith "OUTLOOK.EXE"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count
    from datamodel=Email.All_Email
    where All_Email.file_name!="-"
    by All_Email.src_user, All_Email.recipient, All_Email.file_name, All_Email.subject
| rename All_Email.recipient as user
| join type=inner user
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
        from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
        where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("OUTLOOK.EXE","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe")
          AND Processes.process_name IN ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe")
        by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
     | rename Processes.user as user]
| tstats summariesonly=false count
    from datamodel=Email.All_Email
    where All_Email.file_name!="-"
    by All_Email.src_user, All_Email.recipient, All_Email.file_name, All_Email.subject
| rename All_Email.recipient as user
| join type=inner user
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
        from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
        where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("OUTLOOK.EXE","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe")
          AND Processes.process_name IN ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe")
        by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
     | rename Processes.user as user]

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2", "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea") or SHA1 in~ ("e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2", "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea") or MD5 in~ ("e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2", "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2", "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea") or SHA1 in~ ("e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2", "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea") or MD5 in~ ("e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2", "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2", "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.

| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-24858")

| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) Actions on Objectives High
Mimikatz, comsvcs.dll MiniDump, or any non-Windows process opening LSASS.
ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: LSASS Process Access Outside Microsoft-Signed Tooling
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d04
status: experimental
description: |
    Anything opening lsass.exe with read-memory access that isn't a
    Microsoft signed component (Defender, csrss, etc.) is suspect.
    Mimikatz / Pypykatz / nanodump signature.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1003/001/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1003.001
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: process_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        TargetImage|endswith: '\lsass.exe'
    filter_known_good:
        SourceImage|endswith:
            - '\MsSense.exe'
            - '\MsMpEng.exe'
            - '\csrss.exe'
            - '\svchost.exe'
            - '\wininit.exe'
            - '\services.exe'
            - '\lsm.exe'
            - '\SearchProtocolHost.exe'
    condition: selection and not filter_known_good
falsepositives:
    - Third-party EDR / forensic tools — extend the filter list
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where (Processes.process="*lsass*" OR Processes.process="*sekurlsa*"
        OR Processes.process="*MiniDump*" OR Processes.process="*comsvcs.dll*MiniDump*"
        OR Processes.process="*procdump*lsass*")
       OR (Processes.process_name="rundll32.exe" AND Processes.process="*comsvcs*MiniDump*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
   or (FileName =~ "wmic.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "/node:")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |
    PsExec, paexec, smbexec, csexec, remcom — admin-grade tooling that
    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1021.002
    - attack.t1569.002
    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_psexec:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'
    selection_wmic_remote:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\wmic.exe'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/node:'
    condition: selection_psexec or selection_wmic_remote
falsepositives:
    - Sysadmins running approved remote-management tooling — wrap with
      a known-admin-host watchlist

CISA KEV: CVE-2026-23760 — SmarterTools SmarterMail Authentication Bypass Using an Alternate Path or Channel Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-01-26 4 use cases 2 techniques 3 kill-chain phases detected

SmarterTools SmarterMail contains an authentication bypass using an alternate path or channel vulnerability in the password reset API. The force-reset-password endpoint permits anonymous requests and fails to verify the existing password or a reset token when resetting system administrator accounts. This could allow an unauthenticated attacker to supply a target administrator username and a new password to reset the account, resulting in full administrative compromise of the SmarterMail instance. Vendor: SmarterTools, Product: SmarterMail. Known ransomware use: Known. Federal patch due: 2026-02-16.

Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents

| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc

Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents

| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")

          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1003/001/
author: Clankerusecase

    category: process_access
    product: windows

    selection:
        TargetImage|endswith: '\lsass.exe'
    filter_known_good:
        SourceImage|endswith:
            - '\MsSense.exe'
            - '\MsMpEng.exe'
            - '\csrss.exe'
            - '\svchost.exe'
            - '\wininit.exe'
            - '\services.exe'
            - '\lsm.exe'
            - '\SearchProtocolHost.exe'
    condition: selection and not filter_known_good
falsepositives:
    - Third-party EDR / forensic tools — extend the filter list
level: high

[LLM] DynoWiper deployment from shared inetpub\pub directory (Sandworm, Poland Dec 2025) Installation High
Hunts for the three DynoWiper SHA-1s and their staging filenames (schtask.exe, schtask2.exe, *_update.exe) executing from or being written to a shared directory. ESET observed the wiper deployed to C:\inetpub\pub\ on the victim domain on 29 Dec 2025 and run directly from that share rather than via GPO push. Rationale: Uses the three SHA-1s ESET published (4EC3C9..., 86596A..., 69EDE7...), the unusual staging directory C:\inetpub\pub\ where Sandworm dropped the binaries, and the masquerading filenames schtask.exe/schtask2.exe (impersonating Windows schtasks.exe). All three pivots are confirmed by the ESET technical follow-up and BleepingComputer coverage. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/dynowiper-update-technical-analysis-attribution/ • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/sandworm-hackers-linked-to-failed-wiper-attack-on-polands-energy-systems/ • https://thehackernews.com/2026/01/new-dynowiper-malware-used-in-attempted.html
ATT&CKT1485 T1036.005 T1021.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
let DynoWiperSHA1 = dynamic(["4EC3C90846AF6B79EE1A5188EEFA3FD21F6D4CF6","86596A5C5B05A8BFBD14876DE7404702F7D0D61B","69EDE7E341FD26FA0577692B601D80CB44778D93"]);
union isfuzzy=true
( DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where SHA1 in~ (DynoWiperSHA1)
       or InitiatingProcessSHA1 in~ (DynoWiperSHA1)
       or (FolderPath has @"\inetpub\pub\" and FileName endswith ".exe" and FileName !in~ ("w3wp.exe","appcmd.exe"))
       or (FileName in~ ("schtask.exe","schtask2.exe") and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Windows\")
       or (FileName endswith "_update.exe" and FolderPath has @"\inetpub\")
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA1,
              ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
),
( DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where SHA1 in~ (DynoWiperSHA1)
       or (FolderPath has @"\inetpub\pub\" and FileName endswith ".exe" and ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileRenamed","FileModified"))
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA1,
              ProcessCommandLine="(file_event)", InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
)
| order by Timestamp desc
let DynoWiperSHA1 = dynamic(["4EC3C90846AF6B79EE1A5188EEFA3FD21F6D4CF6","86596A5C5B05A8BFBD14876DE7404702F7D0D61B","69EDE7E341FD26FA0577692B601D80CB44778D93"]);
union isfuzzy=true
( SecurityEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where EventID == 4688
    | where FileHash has_any (DynoWiperSHA1)
       or (NewProcessName endswith "\\schtask.exe" and NewProcessName !contains "\\System32\\")
       or NewProcessName endswith "\\schtask2.exe"
       or NewProcessName matches regex @"(?i)\\inetpub\\pub\\[^\\]+\.exe$"
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine, ParentProcessName, FileHash
),
( ImFileEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where TargetFileSHA1 in~ (DynoWiperSHA1)
       or (TargetFilePath has @"\inetpub\pub\" and TargetFileName endswith ".exe")
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer=DvcHostname, Account=ActorUsername,
              NewProcessName=TargetFilePath, CommandLine=ActingProcessCommandLine,
              ParentProcessName=ActingProcessName, FileHash=TargetFileSHA1
)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

description: Detects execution of the DynoWiper data-wiper used by Sandworm against a Polish energy company on 29 Dec 2025, by SHA-1 hash and the staging filenames/paths observed by ESET.

[LLM] rsocx SOCKS5 reverse proxy beacon to 31.172.71.5:8008 (Sandworm Poland C2) Command & Control High
Hunts for the rsocx tunnelling tool Sandworm staged in the Polish energy intrusion: a binary executed from a user Downloads folder with the argument '-r 31.172.71.5:8008', and any host-to-IP egress to 31.172.71.5 (Fornex Hosting). Rationale: Anchors on the exact rsocx cmdline syntax 'r.exe -r 31.172.71.5:8008' and the C2 IP 31.172.71.5 (Fornex Hosting) ESET published in the technical follow-up. Both the Downloads-folder staging path and the unusual port 8008 give a near-zero FP profile. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/dynowiper-update-technical-analysis-attribution/ • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/sandworm-hackers-linked-to-failed-wiper-attack-on-polands-energy-systems/

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High

ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6") or SHA1 in~ ("4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6") or MD5 in~ ("4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6") or SHA1 in~ ("4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6") or MD5 in~ ("4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs · Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process · OAuth consent / suspicious app grant
[LLM] G_Wagon npm postinstall spawns python with stdin pipe (fileless payload exec) Installation High
Hunts the v1.4.0+ G_Wagon execution pattern where node.exe (running an npm postinstall hook for ansi-universal-ui) launches a locally-extracted python binary with only '-' as argument so the payload is piped via stdin and never touches disk. Filters out python binaries living in standard install roots. Rationale: Anchors on the v1.4.0+ G_Wagon exec primitive verbatim from the article: spawn(LOCAL_PYTHON_BIN, ['-'], { stdio: ['pipe', 'inherit', 'inherit'] }). node.exe parent + python child + lone '-' argument + non-system python path is a near-deterministic signature for fileless npm-dropper payloads.
ATT&CKT1059.006 T1195.002 T1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName =~ "node.exe"
| where FileName matches regex @"(?i)^python(w|3)?\.exe$"
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)python(w|3)?(\.exe)?""?\s+-\s*$"
| where FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Program Files\Python"
   and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Program Files (x86)\Python"
   and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Python"
   and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Windows"
   and FolderPath !contains @"\Anaconda"
   and FolderPath !contains @"\Miniconda"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FolderPath, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessParentFileName, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc

   and ActingProcessName !contains @"\Anaconda"
