| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: G_Wagon npm Package Fileless Python Execution via Node Stdin Pipe
id: 9c3a2b14-5d4f-4a7e-bc31-7d2f8a9e0b21
description: Detects node.exe (npm postinstall hook) spawning a downloaded python binary with only '-' as argument, reading the payload from stdin. Pattern used by the ansi-universal-ui G_Wagon stealer (v1.4.0+) to keep the Python payload memory-only.
references:
  - https://www.aikido.dev/blog/npm-malware-g-wagon-python-stealer-crypto-wallets
author: Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21
tags:
  - attack.execution
  - attack.t1059.006
  - attack.t1195.002
  - attack.t1027
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: windows
detection:
  selection:
    ParentImage|endswith: '\node.exe'
    Image|endswith:
      - '\python.exe'
      - '\pythonw.exe'
      - '\python3.exe'
    CommandLine|re: '(?i)python(w|3)?(\.exe)?"?\s+-\s*$'
  filter_legit_path:
    Image|startswith:
      - 'C:\Program Files\Python'
      - 'C:\Program Files (x86)\Python'
      - 'C:\Python'
  filter_legit_envs:
    Image|contains:
      - '\Anaconda'
      - '\Miniconda'
      - '\conda\'
  condition: selection and not 1 of filter_legit_*
level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:1 @ParentImage:(*\\node.exe OR *\\Node.exe OR *\\NODE.EXE) @Image:(*\\python.exe OR *\\Python.exe OR *\\PYTHON.EXE OR *\\pythonw.exe OR *\\python3.exe) @CommandLine:(*python.exe\ \- OR *python.exe\"\ \- OR *pythonw.exe\ \- OR *python3.exe\ \-) -@Image:(*\\Program\ Files\\Python* OR *\\Anaconda* OR *\\Miniconda*)
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 ParentBaseFileName=/node\.exe$/i ImageFileName=/\\(python|pythonw|python3)\.exe$/i
| regex("(?i)python(w|3)?(\\.exe)?\"?\\s+-\\s*$", field=CommandLine)
| NOT ImageFileName=/\\(Program Files|Python\d+|Anaconda|Miniconda)\\/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ImageFileName, CommandLine, ParentImageFileName], function=count())
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats summariesonly=true count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where Processes.parent_process_name="node.exe" Processes.process_name IN ("python.exe","pythonw.exe","python3.exe") (Processes.process="*python.exe\" -" OR Processes.process="*python.exe -" OR Processes.process="*pythonw.exe\" -" OR Processes.process="*python3.exe\" -") by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.parent_process Processes.process Processes.process_path Processes.parent_process_path | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | where NOT match(process_path, "(?i)\\\\(Program Files|Python\\d+|Anaconda|Miniconda|conda)\\\\") | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where Processes.parent_process_name="node.exe" Processes.process_name IN ("python.exe","pythonw.exe","python3.exe") (Processes.process="*python.exe\" -" OR Processes.process="*python.exe -" OR Processes.process="*pythonw.exe\" -" OR Processes.process="*python3.exe\" -") by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.parent_process Processes.process Processes.process_path Processes.parent_process_path | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | where NOT match(process_path, "(?i)\\\\(Program Files|Python\\d+|Anaconda|Miniconda|conda)\\\\") | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
[LLM] G_Wagon dropper: node.exe spawns system tar.exe extracting from stdin (-x -f - -C) Delivery High
Hunts the G_Wagon delivery primitive where node.exe (npm postinstall for ansi-universal-ui) drops the npm tar dependency and instead pipes an HTTPS response into the Windows built-in tar.exe via stdin to extract a Python runtime tarball. The '-f -' (read archive from stdin) + '-C <cachedir>' combo spawned by node.exe is highly unusual outside this campaign. Rationale: Verbatim from the article diff: spawn('tar', ['-x', '-f', '-', '-C', CACHE_DIR]) replacing require('tar').extract piped from an HTTPS response. node.exe → tar.exe with '-f -' (stdin source) + '-C <dir>' is essentially never seen in benign builds; it's the campaign's intentional dependency-shedding move and a strong primary IOC.
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.007 T1140 T1105
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName =~ "node.exe"
| where FileName =~ "tar.exe"
| where ProcessCommandLine has "-x" and ProcessCommandLine has "-C"
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\s-f\s+-(\s|$)"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FolderPath, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessParentFileName, InitiatingProcessParentCreationTime, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ParentProcessName endswith @"\node.exe"
| where ActingProcessName endswith @"\tar.exe"
| where ActingProcessCommandLine has "-x" and ActingProcessCommandLine has "-C"
| where ActingProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\s-f\s+-(\s|$)"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,
          ActingProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: G_Wagon npm Dropper Spawns System tar.exe Reading From Stdin
id: 7e5b4c91-2a3d-4f8b-9c12-6e8d3a5f7b91
description: Detects node.exe (npm postinstall hook) spawning the Windows built-in tar.exe with stdin extraction flags ('-x -f - -C <dir>'). Used by the ansi-universal-ui G_Wagon dropper to extract a Python runtime tarball streamed from Appwrite without an npm tar dependency, reducing detection surface.
references:
  - https://www.aikido.dev/blog/npm-malware-g-wagon-python-stealer-crypto-wallets
author: Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21
tags:
  - attack.initial_access
  - attack.t1195.002
  - attack.execution
  - attack.t1059.007
  - attack.t1105
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: windows
detection:
  selection:
    ParentImage|endswith: '\node.exe'
    Image|endswith: '\tar.exe'
    CommandLine|contains|all:
      - '-x'
      - '-C'
    CommandLine|re: '(?i)\s-f\s+-(\s|$)'
  condition: selection
level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:1 @ParentImage:(*\\node.exe OR *\\Node.exe OR *\\NODE.EXE) @Image:(*\\tar.exe OR *\\Tar.exe OR *\\TAR.EXE) @CommandLine:(*\-x* AND *\-C* AND (*\-f\ \-\ * OR *\-f\ \-))
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 ParentBaseFileName=/node\.exe$/i ImageFileName=/\\tar\.exe$/i
| regex("(?i)\\s-x(\\s|$)", field=CommandLine)
| regex("(?i)\\s-f\\s+-(\\s|$)", field=CommandLine)
| regex("(?i)\\s-C(\\s|$)", field=CommandLine)
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, CommandLine, ParentImageFileName], function=count())
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats summariesonly=true count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where Processes.parent_process_name="node.exe" Processes.process_name="tar.exe" Processes.process="*-x*" Processes.process="*-f*-*" Processes.process="*-C*" by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.parent_process Processes.process Processes.process_path Processes.parent_process_path | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | regex process="(?i)\s-f\s+-\s+" | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where Processes.parent_process_name="node.exe" Processes.process_name="tar.exe" Processes.process="*-x*" Processes.process="*-f*-*" Processes.process="*-C*" by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.parent_process Processes.process Processes.process_path Processes.parent_process_path | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | regex process="(?i)\s-f\s+-\s+" | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
[LLM] G_Wagon C2 beacon: node.exe or python.exe egress to Appwrite storage buckets Command & Control High
Hunts the G_Wagon command-and-control / exfiltration channel: node.exe (npm dropper) downloading the base64 Python payload from an Appwrite storage bucket, and the dropped python.exe later exfiltrating stolen browser / wallet / cloud creds back to the same Appwrite tenant. The hard-coded bucket / project / file IDs from v1.3.5+ act as a high-fidelity signature. Rationale: Anchors on the verbatim C2 URL from v1.3.5+: https://nyc.cloud.appwrite.io/v1/storage/buckets/688625a0000f8a1b71e8/files/69732d9c000042399d88/view?project=6886229e003d46469fab, plus the v1.4.0+ pivot to fra.cloud.appwrite.io. The three opaque IDs (bucket / file / project) won't collide with benign Appwrite tenants and turn this into a near-IOC-grade alert; the broader domain match catches future ID rotations.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1102.002 T1567.002 T1132.001
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Web.Web
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","python.exe","pythonw.exe","python3.exe")
| where RemoteUrl has "cloud.appwrite.io"
   or RemoteUrl has "688625a0000f8a1b71e8"   // G_Wagon storage bucket
   or RemoteUrl has "6886229e003d46469fab"   // G_Wagon Appwrite project
   or RemoteUrl has "69732d9c000042399d88"   // G_Wagon payload file ID
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessParentFileName, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Protocol
| order by Timestamp desc
let ApwIOC = dynamic(["nyc.cloud.appwrite.io","fra.cloud.appwrite.io","688625a0000f8a1b71e8","6886229e003d46469fab","69732d9c000042399d88"]);
(union isfuzzy=true
  (ImWebSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where Url has_any (ApwIOC) or DstHostname has_any (ApwIOC)
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, SrcIpAddr, SrcUsername, DstHostname, Url, HttpMethod, HttpStatusCode, HttpRequestBodyBytes, HttpResponseBodyBytes),
  (ImDnsActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where DnsQuery has_any ("nyc.cloud.appwrite.io","fra.cloud.appwrite.io")
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, SrcIpAddr, SrcUsername, DnsQuery, DstIpAddr=DnsResponseName,
              Url="", HttpMethod="", HttpStatusCode=int(null), HttpRequestBodyBytes=long(null), HttpResponseBodyBytes=long(null))
)
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: G_Wagon Stealer C2 Beacon to Appwrite Storage From Node or Python
id: 4d2e8f6a-9b3c-4e51-a8d4-2f7e9b1c5a83
description: Detects outbound HTTPS to nyc.cloud.appwrite.io / fra.cloud.appwrite.io storage endpoints (or the hard-coded G_Wagon bucket / project / file IDs) initiated by node.exe or a python binary, matching the ansi-universal-ui (G_Wagon) C2 + exfiltration channel.
references:

author: Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21

[LLM] G_Wagon Python runtime drop into npm cache with lib_core/renderer or python_runtime paths Installation Medium
Hunts the file-system footprint left by the G_Wagon dropper as system tar.exe (spawned by node.exe) extracts a portable Python interpreter into a per-user npm cache directory. The article calls out two obfuscation generations of the runtime folder name: 'python_runtime' (early) and 'lib_core/renderer' (v1.4.1+). Catching the directory creation gives an artifact even when the payload itself stays in memory. Rationale: Article explicitly names 'python_runtime' (v1.3.x) renamed to 'lib_core/renderer' (v1.4.1+) and LOCAL_SCRIPT_PATH = path.join(CACHE_DIR, 'latest_script.py') in v1.3.5–v1.3.7. Combining the directory string with the tar.exe / node.exe writer process limits FPs to legitimate npm packages that happen to ship those exact directory names, which is essentially nil outside this campaign.
ATT&CKT1105 T1036.005 T1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileRenamed","FileModified")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("tar.exe","node.exe")
| where FolderPath matches regex @"(?i)\\(lib_core\\renderer|python_runtime)\\"
   or FileName =~ "latest_script.py"
   or (FileName matches regex @"(?i)^python(w|3)?\.exe$"
        and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Program Files"
        and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Python"
        and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Windows"
        and FolderPath !contains @"\Anaconda"
        and FolderPath !contains @"\Miniconda")
| summarize FirstSeen=min(Timestamp), LastSeen=max(Timestamp),
            FileCount=count(), SampleFiles=make_set(FileName, 20),
            SampleFolder=any(FolderPath)
            by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName,
               InitiatingProcessParentFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by FirstSeen desc
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where ActingProcessName endswith_cs @"\tar.exe" or ActingProcessName endswith_cs @"\node.exe"
| where TargetFilePath matches regex @"(?i)\\(lib_core\\renderer|python_runtime)\\"
   or TargetFileName =~ "latest_script.py"
   or (TargetFileName matches regex @"(?i)^python(w|3)?\.exe$"
        and TargetFilePath !startswith @"C:\Program Files"
        and TargetFilePath !startswith @"C:\Python"
        and TargetFilePath !contains @"\Anaconda")
| summarize FirstSeen=min(TimeGenerated), LastSeen=max(TimeGenerated),
            FileCount=count(), SampleFiles=make_set(TargetFileName, 20),
            SampleFolder=any(TargetFilePath)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine
| order by FirstSeen desc
title: G_Wagon Python Runtime Dropped into npm Cache (lib_core/renderer or python_runtime)
id: 8a1f3e72-4c6b-4d92-9f15-3b8e2d5a7c91
description: Detects file writes by tar.exe or node.exe into directories named lib_core\renderer or python_runtime, or a file named latest_script.py — the article-documented runtime / payload drop paths for the ansi-universal-ui G_Wagon npm stealer.
references:
  - https://www.aikido.dev/blog/npm-malware-g-wagon-python-stealer-crypto-wallets
author: Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21
tags:
  - attack.execution
  - attack.t1105
  - attack.t1036.005
  - attack.t1027
logsource:
  category: file_event
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_writer:
    Image|endswith:
      - '\tar.exe'
      - '\node.exe'
  selection_path:
    TargetFilename|contains:
      - '\lib_core\renderer\'
      - '\python_runtime\'
  selection_file:
    TargetFilename|endswith:
      - '\latest_script.py'
  condition: selection_writer and (selection_path or selection_file)
level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:11 @Image:(*\\tar.exe OR *\\Tar.exe OR *\\TAR.EXE OR *\\node.exe OR *\\Node.exe OR *\\NODE.EXE) @TargetFilename:(*\\lib_core\\renderer\\* OR *\\python_runtime\\* OR *\\latest_script.py)
#event_simpleName=FileWritten ContextImageFileName=/\\(tar|node)\.exe$/i
| TargetFileName=/(\\lib_core\\renderer\\|\\python_runtime\\|\\latest_script\.py$)/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ContextImageFileName, TargetFileName], function=count())

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies

references:

date: 2026/05/02

    category: file_access

            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'

            - '\chrome.exe'

        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"

id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d08
status: experimental
description: |
    PowerShell launched with -EncodedCommand, base64 strings, hidden
    window, no-profile, bypass policy, IEX / Invoke-Expression / Net
    .WebClient.DownloadString — combinations indicating evasion.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/

date: 2026/05/02

    category: process_creation
    product: windows

    selection_image:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'

        CommandLine|contains:
            - '-enc '
            - 'EncodedCommand'
            - 'FromBase64String'
            - 'Invoke-Expression'
            - 'IEX('
            - 'DownloadString'
            - 'Net.WebClient'
            - '-w hidden'
            - '-WindowStyle Hidden'
            - '-nop'
            - '-NoProfile'
            - '-ep bypass'
            - '-ExecutionPolicy Bypass'
    condition: selection_image and selection_args

      cross-shell escaping

        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64String*" OR Processes.process="*-nop*"
        OR Processes.process="*-w hidden*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*IEX(*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*Net.WebClient*")

| tstats summariesonly=false count earliest(_time) AS firstTime latest(_time) AS lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where (Processes.process_name IN ("latest_script.py","index.js") OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name,
       Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_path
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| `security_content_ctime(firstTime)`
| append [
| tstats summariesonly=false count
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("created","modified")
      AND (Filesystem.file_name IN ("latest_script.py","index.js"))
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, Filesystem.process_name,
       Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.file_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
]
High

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] Aikido npm phishing: DNS / web request to siemens-energy.icu or siemensergy.icu typosquats · [LLM] Aikido campaign: jsDelivr CDN fetch of weaponised flockiali/opresc/prndn/oprnm/operni npm package · [LLM] Aikido npm phishing: user clicked phishing URL hosting /DIVzTaSF credential capture · [LLM] Aikido npm phishing: inbound email containing jsDelivr link to flockiali/opresc/prndn/oprnm/operni · Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution · Email attachment opened from external sender
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Office app spawning script/LOLBin child process · Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha) · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — Gone Phishin': npm Packages Serving Custom Credential Harvesting Pages
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Aikido npm phishing: direct outbound connection to RackGenius C2 (163.123.236.118) · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process · Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs
[LLM] Aikido npm phishing: direct outbound connection to RackGenius C2 (163.123.236.118) Command & Control High
Detects any endpoint making a direct outbound network connection to the Aikido-reported credential-harvesting C2 IP 163.123.236.118 (RackGenius, Muskegon MI), which fronts login.siemens-energy[.]icu / login.siemensergy[.]icu. This is article-specific because the IP was used exclusively by the flockiali/opresc/prndn/oprnm/operni npm phishing campaign in January 2026. Rationale: 163.123.236.118 is the dedicated RackGenius C2 IP Aikido tied to the campaign; corroborated via certificate transparency timeline (Oct 2025 cert on *.siemens-energy.icu pointing here). Any outbound to this IP is high-fidelity TP with negligible FP risk. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/npm-supply-chain-phishing-campaigns • https://crt.sh/?q=siemens-energy.icu
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1566.002

DeviceNetworkEvents

| where RemoteIP == "163.123.236.118"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl, Protocol
| order by Timestamp desc
(union isfuzzy=true
    (ImNetworkSession
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
        | where DstIpAddr == "163.123.236.118"
        | project TimeGenerated, SrcIpAddr, SrcHostname, SrcUsername, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber, NetworkProtocol, Url, EventVendor, EventProduct),
    (CommonSecurityLog
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
        | where DestinationIP == "163.123.236.118"
        | project TimeGenerated, SourceIP, SourceHostName, SourceUserName, DestinationIP, DestinationPort, ApplicationProtocol, RequestURL, DeviceVendor, DeviceProduct)
)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Aikido campaign: jsDelivr CDN fetch of weaponised flockiali/opresc/prndn/oprnm/operni npm package Delivery High
Detects HTTP fetch of the malicious npm packages via jsDelivr CDN (cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/<pkg>@<ver>/resp/template.min.js or .../reps/template.min.js). These five package names had zero benign usage and were registered solely to host the credential-harvest payload. Rationale: All five package names are explicitly named in the Aikido report and confirmed as dedicated phishing-payload carriers; jsDelivr automatically mirrors them at the listed paths. No legitimate npm consumer would request these five specific packages. Verified via npm registry pages (now removed) and Deno takedown of oprsys.deno.dev. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/npm-supply-chain-phishing-campaigns • https://www.npmjs.com/package/flockiali • https://www.jsdelivr.com/package/npm/flockiali

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
Email attachment opened from external sender Delivery High
Attachment delivery + execution by recipient — most common malware initial access.
ATT&CKT1566.001 T1204.002
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Endpoint.Processes EmailAttachmentInfo DeviceProcessEvents
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MalAttachments = EmailAttachmentInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | project NetworkMessageId, RecipientEmailAddress,
              AttachmentFileName = FileName, AttachmentSHA256 = SHA256;
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("OUTLOOK.EXE","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                      "mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe")
| join kind=inner MalAttachments on $left.AccountUpn == $right.RecipientEmailAddress
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, AttachmentFileName, AttachmentSHA256
// Sentinel doesn't have an attachment-hash table. Best approximation:
// OfficeActivity Send/Receive activity + correlate to host process spawn.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MailWithAttach = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation in~ ("Send","Receive")
    | where isnotempty(MessageId)
    | project EmailTime = TimeGenerated, MailboxOwnerUPN, Sender,
              Recipients, Subject, MessageId;
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName endswith "OUTLOOK.EXE"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc
ENDPOINT

| rename All_Email.recipient as user
| join type=inner user
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count

     | rename Processes.user as user]

    from datamodel=Email.All_Email
    where All_Email.file_name!="-"
    by All_Email.src_user, All_Email.recipient, All_Email.file_name, All_Email.subject
| rename All_Email.recipient as user
| join type=inner user

title: FakeCaptcha / ClickFix - Clipboard-Injected PowerShell from Explorer
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d06
status: experimental
description: |
    "FakeCaptcha" / "ClickFix" social-engineering: malicious site asks
    user to press Win+R and paste a payload. The result is
    powershell/mshta launched directly from explorer.exe with iex /
    base64 / downloadstring in the command line.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1204/002/
author: Clankerusecase

    - attack.t1204.002

    category: process_creation
    product: windows

        ParentImage|endswith:
            - '\explorer.exe'
            - '\RuntimeBroker.exe'

        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
    selection_cmd:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - 'iex'
            - 'invoke-expression'
            - 'frombase64'
            - 'downloadstring'
            - 'hxxp'

            - 'wget '

      AND (Processes.process="*iex*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*hxxp*" OR Processes.process="*curl*" OR Processes.process="*wget*")

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("3ceb182fb32a8fb0f0fcf056d6ab8de1cf6e789053f1aadc98ba315ae9a96f0c", "fdb6c79a8d01b528698c53ebd5030f875242e6af93f6ae799dee7f66b452bf3e", "4631584783d84758ae58bc717b08ac67d99dee30985db18b9d2b08df8721348e", "211f88a55e8fe9254f75c358c42bb7e78e014b862de7ea6e8b80ed1f78d13add", "7d7f795ac1fcb5623731a50999f518877fd423a5a98219d0f495c488564a1554") or SHA1 in~ ("3ceb182fb32a8fb0f0fcf056d6ab8de1cf6e789053f1aadc98ba315ae9a96f0c", "fdb6c79a8d01b528698c53ebd5030f875242e6af93f6ae799dee7f66b452bf3e", "4631584783d84758ae58bc717b08ac67d99dee30985db18b9d2b08df8721348e", "211f88a55e8fe9254f75c358c42bb7e78e014b862de7ea6e8b80ed1f78d13add", "7d7f795ac1fcb5623731a50999f518877fd423a5a98219d0f495c488564a1554") or MD5 in~ ("3ceb182fb32a8fb0f0fcf056d6ab8de1cf6e789053f1aadc98ba315ae9a96f0c", "fdb6c79a8d01b528698c53ebd5030f875242e6af93f6ae799dee7f66b452bf3e", "4631584783d84758ae58bc717b08ac67d99dee30985db18b9d2b08df8721348e", "211f88a55e8fe9254f75c358c42bb7e78e014b862de7ea6e8b80ed1f78d13add", "7d7f795ac1fcb5623731a50999f518877fd423a5a98219d0f495c488564a1554")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("3ceb182fb32a8fb0f0fcf056d6ab8de1cf6e789053f1aadc98ba315ae9a96f0c", "fdb6c79a8d01b528698c53ebd5030f875242e6af93f6ae799dee7f66b452bf3e", "4631584783d84758ae58bc717b08ac67d99dee30985db18b9d2b08df8721348e", "211f88a55e8fe9254f75c358c42bb7e78e014b862de7ea6e8b80ed1f78d13add", "7d7f795ac1fcb5623731a50999f518877fd423a5a98219d0f495c488564a1554") or SHA1 in~ ("3ceb182fb32a8fb0f0fcf056d6ab8de1cf6e789053f1aadc98ba315ae9a96f0c", "fdb6c79a8d01b528698c53ebd5030f875242e6af93f6ae799dee7f66b452bf3e", "4631584783d84758ae58bc717b08ac67d99dee30985db18b9d2b08df8721348e", "211f88a55e8fe9254f75c358c42bb7e78e014b862de7ea6e8b80ed1f78d13add", "7d7f795ac1fcb5623731a50999f518877fd423a5a98219d0f495c488564a1554") or MD5 in~ ("3ceb182fb32a8fb0f0fcf056d6ab8de1cf6e789053f1aadc98ba315ae9a96f0c", "fdb6c79a8d01b528698c53ebd5030f875242e6af93f6ae799dee7f66b452bf3e", "4631584783d84758ae58bc717b08ac67d99dee30985db18b9d2b08df8721348e", "211f88a55e8fe9254f75c358c42bb7e78e014b862de7ea6e8b80ed1f78d13add", "7d7f795ac1fcb5623731a50999f518877fd423a5a98219d0f495c488564a1554")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["3ceb182fb32a8fb0f0fcf056d6ab8de1cf6e789053f1aadc98ba315ae9a96f0c", "fdb6c79a8d01b528698c53ebd5030f875242e6af93f6ae799dee7f66b452bf3e", "4631584783d84758ae58bc717b08ac67d99dee30985db18b9d2b08df8721348e", "211f88a55e8fe9254f75c358c42bb7e78e014b862de7ea6e8b80ed1f78d13add", "7d7f795ac1fcb5623731a50999f518877fd423a5a98219d0f495c488564a1554"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

CISA KEV: CVE-2024-37079 — Broadcom VMware vCenter Server Out-of-bounds Write Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-01-23 1 use case 1 technique 2 kill-chain phases detected

Broadcom VMware vCenter Server contains an out-of-bounds write vulnerability in the implementation of the DCERPC protocol. This could allow a malicious actor with network access to vCenter Server to send specially crafted network packets, potentially leading to remote code execution. Vendor: Broadcom, Product: VMware vCenter Server. Federal patch due: 2026-02-13.

// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2024-37079")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2024-37079")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

[LLM] PyPI install of malicious typosquat spellcheckpy or spellcheckerpy Delivery High
Hunts pip / uv / pipx install events that pull either of the two PyPI typosquats impersonating pyspellchecker. Catches the delivery phase before the eu.json.gz Basque-dictionary payload is decoded and executed via the SpellChecker import. Rationale: Anchors on the two exact PyPI package names called out in the article (spellcheckpy, spellcheckerpy). Both are typosquats of pyspellchecker; legitimate hits should be limited to malware-research workstations. Cross-checked with the related HelixGuard write-up referenced in the article that describes the same actor's prior 'spellcheckers' campaign. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/malicious-pypi-packages-spellcheckpy-and-spellcheckerpy-deliver-python-rat • https://pypi.org/project/pyspellchecker/
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (InitiatingProcessFileName has_any ("pip.exe","pip3.exe","pip","pip3","uv","uv.exe","pipx","pipx.exe","python.exe","python3","python")
      or FileName has_any ("pip.exe","pip3.exe","uv.exe","pipx.exe"))
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("spellcheckpy","spellcheckerpy")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FolderPath
| order by Timestamp desc

      or ParentProcessName has_any ("pip.exe","uv.exe","pipx.exe","python.exe")
| where TargetUserName !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine, ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Python parent spawns detached 'python3 -' child reading payload from stdin Installation High
Detects the stage-1 fileless downloader pattern in spellcheckpy: a Python interpreter is spawned by another Python process and invoked with the sole '-' argument (read script from stdin), receiving the RAT body through the parent's pipe and surviving via start_new_session=True. Nothing touches disk. Rationale: Verbatim lift from the article's subprocess.Popen(["python3","-"], stdin=subprocess.PIPE, start_new_session=True) snippet. Python-spawns-Python with a sole '-' argument is rare outside of test rigs and uniquely matches the stage-1 RAT loader. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/malicious-pypi-packages-spellcheckpy-and-spellcheckerpy-deliver-python-rat
ATT&CKT1059.006 T1105

      - ' -'

      - '/python3'

[LLM] C2 beacon or stage-2 fetch to updatenet[.]work / 172.86.73.139 / dothebest[.]store Command & Control High
Detects outbound HTTP(S) or DNS traffic to the spellcheckpy C2 domain updatenet.work, the RouterHosting/Cloudzy A-record 172.86.73.139, or the prior-campaign C2 dothebest.store (HelixGuard report). The RAT beacons /update1.php every 5 seconds — a single hit on this triplet is high-fidelity. Rationale: Three IOCs named directly by the article: updatenet.work (live C2 + /update1.php beacon + /settings/history.php stage-2), 172.86.73.139 (RouterHosting/Cloudzy A-record), and dothebest.store (prior-campaign C2 from HelixGuard write-up that the article links the same actor to). Each is unique enough that a single hit is investigatable; ContextImageFileName == python.exe with site-packages/spellchecker* in path is the confirmation pivot. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/malicious-pypi-packages-spellcheckpy-and-spellcheckerpy-deliver-python-rat • https://www.helixguard.io/research • https://www.halcyon.ai/research/cloudzy-with-a-chance-of-ransomware

| order by Timestamp desc
let c2_domains = dynamic(["updatenet.work","dothebest.store"]);
let c2_ip = "172.86.73.139";

  (DnsEvents
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where Name has_any (c2_domains)
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=Computer, ClientIP, Indicator=Name, SourceTable="DnsEvents"),
  (CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where DestinationHostName has_any (c2_domains)
        or DestinationIP == c2_ip
        or RequestURL contains "updatenet.work"
        or RequestURL contains "dothebest.store"
        or RequestURL contains "update1.php"
        or RequestURL contains "settings/history.php"
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=SourceHostName, ClientIP=SourceIP, Indicator=coalesce(DestinationHostName, RequestURL, DestinationIP), SourceTable="CommonSecurityLog"),
  (ImNetworkSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where DstHostname has_any (c2_domains)
        or DstIpAddr == c2_ip
        or Url contains "update1.php"
        or Url contains "settings/history.php"
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=DvcHostname, ClientIP=SrcIpAddr, Indicator=coalesce(DstHostname, DstIpAddr, Url), SourceTable="ImNetworkSession")
| order by TimeGenerated desc

| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files

    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution

    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_psexec:

            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'
    selection_wmic_remote:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\wmic.exe'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/node:'
    condition: selection_psexec or selection_wmic_remote
falsepositives:
    - Sysadmins running approved remote-management tooling — wrap with
      a known-admin-host watchlist
level: high

| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental

    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when

detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'

            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT

CISA KEV
2026-01-21 1 use case 0 techniques 2 kill-chain phases detected

Cisco Unified Communications Manager (Unified CM), Cisco Unified Communications Manager Session Management Edition (Unified CM SME), Cisco Unified Communications Manager IM & Presence Service (Unified CM IM&P), Cisco Unity Connection, and Cisco Webex Calling Dedicated Instance contain a code injection vulnerability that could allow the attacker to obtain user-level access to the underlying operating system and then elevate privileges to root. Vendor: Cisco, Product: Unified Communications Manager. Federal patch due: 2026-02-11.

Actions on Objectives
—
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-20045")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-20045")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc
APPLICATION
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Vulnerabilities
    where Vulnerabilities.signature IN ("CVE-2026-20045")
    by Vulnerabilities.dest, Vulnerabilities.signature, Vulnerabilities.severity, Vulnerabilities.cve
| `drop_dm_object_name(Vulnerabilities)`
| sort - severity
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Vulnerabilities
    where Vulnerabilities.signature IN ("CVE-2026-20045")
    by Vulnerabilities.dest, Vulnerabilities.signature, Vulnerabilities.severity, Vulnerabilities.cve
| `drop_dm_object_name(Vulnerabilities)`
| sort - severity
